is a cycle of well-linked sets of width
w
and length
\ell
.

If the back-linkage intersects most of the clusters of the path of well-linked sets, we need to control how these intersections happen.
In particular, we show that we can obtain a path of well-linked sets and a back-linkage which intersects the clusters from the end to the beginning in a (mostly) ordered fashion.
To describe this property precisely, we use the notion of
jumps
, defined below.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
be a path of well-linked sets of length
\ell
.
A
jump
of length
k
over
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
is a path
R
with
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(R\right)}{(R)}{(R)}{(R)}\in V\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}\right)}{(S_{i})}{(S_{i})}{(S_{i})}\cup V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{i})}{(\mathcal{P}_{i})}{(\mathcal{P}_{i})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(R\right)}{(R)}{(R)}{(R)}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(S_{j}\right)}{(S_{j})}{(S_{j})}{(S_{j})}\cup V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{j}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{j})}{(\mathcal{P}_{j})}{(\mathcal{P}_{j})}
(if
j=\ell
, we require
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(R\right)}{(R)}{(R)}{(R)}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(S_{j}\right)}{(S_{j})}{(S_{j})}{(S_{j})}
instead) such that
|j-i|=k
.
If
i<j
, then
R
is a
forward
jump.
If
i\geq j
and
R
is internally disjoint from
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
, then
R
is a
backward
jump, see
fig.

6
for an illustration.

B_{1}
B_{2}
J_{1}
J_{2}

Figure 6
:
A path of well-linked sets with two backward jumps
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
and two forward jumps
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
.
Note that even if the
dashed blue path
exists, the two jumps cannot be combined into one, as the result would not be internally disjoint from the path of well-linked sets.
This is not required for forward jumps, as
J_{1}
shows.

Note that while a backward jump must be internally disjoint from the path of well-linked sets, we do not require this from a forward jump.
In fact, a forward jump could simply be a subpath of a path
R\in\mathcal{R}
which is fully contained inside the path of well-linked sets
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
.

By excluding forward jumps, we guarantee that the back-linkage must intersect the clusters of a path of well-linked sets in an ordered fashion.
Additionally excluding backward jumps of length greater than one ensures that the back-linkage intersects all clusters.
These properties will be useful later when we construct a web using the back-linkage and the path of well-linked sets.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
be a path of well-linked sets and let
\mathcal{R}
be a partial back-linkage for
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
.
We say that
\mathcal{R}
intersects
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}

cluster-by-cluster
if
\mathcal{R}
does not contain any forward or backward jump of length greater than one over
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
.

Note that even if
\mathcal{R}
intersects
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
cluster-by-cluster, this does not imply that the paths in
\mathcal{R}
visit the clusters strictly in reverse order
S_{\ell},S_{\ell-1},\dots,S_{0}
.
It is still possible that a path in
\mathcal{R}
intersects a cluster
S_{i}
, then goes back to
S_{i+1}
, and then intersects
S_{i}
again.
So the paths in
\mathcal{R}
can go back and forth between two consecutive clusters numerous times.
However, once
R
hits a vertex in
S_{i-1}
, it can no longer go back to
S_{i+1}
.

To obtain a back-linkage intersecting our path of well-linked sets cluster-by-cluster, we start with a back-linkage
\mathcal{R}
which is weakly minimal with respect to the path of well-linked sets.

The weak-minimality property allows us to exclude long forward jumps in the case where we do not have long backward jumps, since we can use the well-linkedness of the clusters to construct a linkage which would contradict the weak-minimality property.

We show how to eliminate backward jumps in the second step, proved in
lemma

4.8
below.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
be a strict
2

Since we can always take a path of well-linked sets to be strict without losing width or height, we often implicitly assume that the path of well-linked sets we construct is strict.

\mathcal{R}
is a jump over
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}
of length
3mj^{\prime}
.
Hence,
\mathcal{R}
does not contain any forward jumps or backward jumps of length greater than one over
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}
.
Finally, the linkage
\mathcal{R}
is a back-linkage for
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}
and
\mathcal{R}
intersects
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}
cluster-by-cluster.

In order to exclude long backward jumps so that we can apply

lemma

4.6
,
we try to construct a cycle of well-linked sets from the backward jumps.
If we have many very long backward jumps, then these jumps essentially act like a back-linkage
in the context of
lemma

4.2
.

We define

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-back-linkage-intersects-cluster-by-cluster}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-back-linkage-intersects-cluster-by-cluster}}}\mathchoice{\left(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m\right)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 3\ell_{2}m((\ell_{1}+3)(3\ell_{2}m)^{w_{1}}+6\frac{(3\ell_{2}m)^{w_{1}}-1}{w_{1}-1}),

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-back-linkage-intersects-cluster-by-cluster}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-back-linkage-intersects-cluster-by-cluster}}}\mathchoice{\left(w_{1},w_{2}\right)}{(w_{1},w_{2})}{(w_{1},w_{2})}{(w_{1},w_{2})}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2w_{2}+w_{1}.

Observe that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-back-linkage-intersects-cluster-by-cluster}}}\mathchoice{\left(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m\right)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{1}(\operatorname{poly}^{2}(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m))
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\ell_{1},w_{1},\ell_{2},w_{2}
be integers, let
(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots S_{\ell^{\prime}}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots S_{\ell^{\prime}})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots S_{\ell^{\prime}})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots S_{\ell^{\prime}})},\mathscr{P}=(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell^{\prime}-1}))
be a strict path of well-linked sets of length
\ell^{\prime}\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-back-linkage-intersects-cluster-by-cluster}}}\mathchoice{\left(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m\right)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},m)}

w_{2}

of order

intersecting it cluster-by-cluster.

4.2
Obtaining a 2-horizontal web

We use the back-linkage intersecting our
path of well-linked sets cluster-by-cluster
in order to construct a new web with additional properties
which we use later in
section

5
.
Intuitively, we are looking for a web
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
which mimics the cluster-by-cluster property.
That is, we want to be able to decompose the
horizontal
linkage
\mathcal{H}
into parts such that
\mathcal{V}
intersects
\mathcal{H}
in a horizontally sorted fashion.
This type of web is called a
horizontal web
and is defined below.
An example is provided in
fig.

8
.

Definition 4.10
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
be a web.
We say that
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
is a
c
-horizontal web
if every path
H_{i}\in\mathcal{H}
can be decomposed into paths
H_{i}=H_{i}^{1}\cdot H_{i}^{2}\cdot\ldots\cdot H_{i}^{c}
and every path
V_{j}\in\mathcal{V}
can be decomposed into paths
V_{j}=V_{j}^{1}\cdot V_{j}^{2}\cdot\ldots\cdot V_{j}^{c}
such that
V_{j}^{x}\cap H_{i}\subseteq H_{i}^{c-x}\cup H_{i}^{c-x+1}
and
V_{j}^{x}\cap H_{i}^{c-x+1}\neq\emptyset
for all
1\leq x\leq c
, where for simplicity we define
H_{i}^{0}
to be empty.

Figure 8
:
A
(4,1)
-web that is a
3
-horizontal web.
Four horizontal paths
H_{1},H_{2},H_{3}
and
H_{4}
partitioned into three subpaths each and one vertical path
V_{1}
partitioned into three subpaths
V_{1}^{1}
,
V_{1}^{2}
and
V_{1}^{3}
.

V_{1}^{1}
only intersects the latter two subpaths of the horizontal paths.

V_{1}^{2}
only intersects the first two, note that it always intersects the second subpaths, but not necessarily the first.
Finally,
V_{1}^{3}
only intersects the first subpath of the horizontal paths.

We start by constructing an ordered web from a back-linkage and a path of well-linked sets.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
be a strict path of well-linked sets of length

and width at least

in a digraph

, and let

be a partial back-linkage of order

intersecting

If

, then there is a linkage

of order

inside

with

Proof 4.12
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}\coloneqq\mathcal{S}

\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}\coloneqq\mathscr{P}
.
To simplify notation, we set
\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\coloneqq\emptyset
.
Since
\mathcal{R}
intersects
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}

intersects some vertex of
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{i}\right)}{(S_{i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{i})}{(S_{i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{i})}{(S_{i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{i})}
for every
0\leq i<\ell
.
Let
\mathcal{R}=\mathchoice{\left(R_{1},R_{2},\dots,R_{r}\right)}{(R_{1},R_{2},\dots,R_{r})}{(R_{1},R_{2},\dots,R_{r})}{(R_{1},R_{2},\dots,R_{r})}

, let

and let

be some vertex in

.
Let

be a path visiting

with

and

.
Since
k^{i}_{j}-k^{i}_{j-1}=2
for all
2\leq j\leq r
, by
lemma

3.5
item

(L4)
there is a
z^{i}_{j}
-

-path

inside
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[k^{i}_{j},k^{i}_{j+1}\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[k^{i}_{j},k^{i}_{j+1}\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[k^{i}_{j},k^{i}_{j+1}\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[k^{i}_{j},k^{i}_{j+1}\right]}
for every
1\leq j<r
.
Since all
V^{i}_{j}
and all
Q^{i}_{j}
are pairwise internally disjoint and
V^{i}_{j}
intersects
R_{j}
at
u^{i}_{j}
, the path
V^{i}=Q^{i}_{1}\cdot V^{i}_{1}\cdot Q^{i}_{2}\cdot V^{i}_{2}\cdot\ldots\cdot Q^{i}_{r}
in
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[k^{i}_{1},k^{i}_{r}\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[k^{i}_{1},k^{i}_{r}\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[k^{i}_{1},k^{i}_{r}\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[k^{i}_{1},k^{i}_{r}\right]}
intersects every path in
\mathcal{R}
.

Let
\mathcal{V}=\{V^{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq v\}
.
Since
k^{i}_{1}-k^{i-1}_{r}=2
for all
2\leq i\leq v
, all paths in
\mathcal{V}
are pairwise disjoint.
Further, such paths exist because
\ell\geq k^{v}_{r}=2(r-1)+2r(v-1)
.
Finally, because
\mathcal{R}
intersects
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
cluster-by-cluster,
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{V})}
is an ordered web.

Our goal is to obtain a 2-horizontal web
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
where
\mathcal{H}
is weakly
|\mathcal{H}|
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
.
The idea is that
\mathcal{H}
goes
forwards
through the path of well-linked sets
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
from beginning to end, that is,
in the same direction as the path of well-linked sets.

(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
contains a forward linkage from its beginning to its end, and as a result of the way the linkage
\mathcal{V}
is constructed, we can construct
\mathcal{H}
so that it forms a web together with
\mathcal{V}
.
However, if we naively choose
\mathcal{H}
as a
\mathcal{V}
-minimal linkage,
then we may lose intersections between
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{V}
, and the result would
no longer be a 2-horizontal web.

To address this issue, we need to be able to construct a cycle of well-linked sets in the case
where many paths of
\mathcal{H}
become disjoint from many paths in
\mathcal{V}
.
In
lemma

4.15
, we show that
a path of well-linked sets which contains a
forward linkage
which is disjoint from
the back-linkage also contains a cycle of well-linked sets.

Towards proving
lemma

4.15
,
we show in
lemma

4.13
below that a path of well-linked sets which contains a
forward linkage

\mathcal{L}

can be split in such a way that
some
\mathcal{L}^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{L}
is disjoint from a smaller path of well-linked sets.

Proof 4.14
.

Let

\ell_{1}=w\ell
,

.

We show that every layer of

is strongly connected.

be two distinct paths.

\mathcal{L}
intersects every cluster in
\mathcal{S}
, there is some

.

Proof 4.16
.

Let

\ell_{4}=\ell-1
,

\ell_{3}=4w+1
,

,

We consider a relaxation of horizontal webs where we no longer require
that every path in one linkage intersects every path
in the other linkage, which we call a
c
-horizontal semi-web
.
Observe that every
c
-horizontal web is also a
c
-horizontal semi-web.

Definition 4.17
.

Let
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}
be two linkages.
We say that
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
is a
c
-
horizontal semi-web
if
\mathcal{H}
can be decomposed as
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2}\cdot\ldots\cdot\mathcal{H}^{c}
and
\mathcal{V}
can be decomposed as
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{2}\cdot\ldots\cdot\mathcal{V}^{c}
such that
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}^{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{V}^{i})}{(\mathcal{V}^{i})}{(\mathcal{V}^{i})}\cap\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}\subseteq\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{c-i+1}\cup\mathcal{V}^{c-i}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{c-i+1}\cup\mathcal{V}^{c-i})}{(\mathcal{H}^{c-i+1}\cup\mathcal{V}^{c-i})}{(\mathcal{H}^{c-i+1}\cup\mathcal{V}^{c-i})}
(we set
\mathcal{V}^{0}=\emptyset
for simplicity).

While choosing the linkage
\mathcal{H}
to be weakly minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
in a horizontal web may produce a semi-web instead, we can still preserve the
horizontal
property of the semi-web, as shown below.

Observation 4.2
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
be a
3
-horizontal semi-web in a digraph
D
.
Let
\mathcal{H}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{3}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}
and

\mathcal{V}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{3}\coloneqq\mathcal{V}

be decompositions of these two linkages witnessing that
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
is a 3-horizontal semi-web.
Let
\mathcal{U}^{2}=\mathcal{V}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{3}
.
Then
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}\right)}{(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})})}{(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})})}{(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})})}
contains a linkage
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{P}^{2}
of order
|\mathcal{H}|
and
such that

\mathcal{P}
is weakly
|\mathcal{H}|
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
,

\mathcal{P}^{2}
is internally disjoint from
\mathcal{U}^{2}
,

\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}=\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
,

\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
and

\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
.

Proof 4.18
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
-
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
-linkage of order
|\mathcal{H}|
which is
\mathcal{V}
-minimal.
By
2.4
,
\mathcal{P}
is also weakly
|\mathcal{H}|
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
.

Since
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
is a 3-horizontal semi-web, there is no path from
\mathcal{H}^{1}
to
\mathcal{H}^{3}
in
\mathcal{V}
.
As
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}=\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}=\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{1})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{3}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{3})}{(\mathcal{H}^{3})}{(\mathcal{H}^{3})}
, every path in
\mathcal{P}
must intersect some vertex of
\mathcal{H}^{2}
.
Let
Y
be the set containing, for each
P\in\mathcal{P}
, the last vertex of
P
which is also in
\mathcal{H}^{2}
.

Decompose
\mathcal{P}
into
\mathcal{P}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{P}^{2}=\mathcal{P}
such that
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}=Y
.
By construction, we have that
\mathcal{P}^{2}
is internally disjoint from
\mathcal{U}^{2}
.

Using
4.2
above we can
show that we can construct a horizontal web
(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})

from a semi-web
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})

such that
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is weakly minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
, or
we find large
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{V}

which are disjoint.
From the latter case we will construct a cycle of well-linked sets in

lemma

4.21
,
whereas the former case will be handled in
section

5
.

We define

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h_{1},h_{2}\right)}{(h_{1},h_{2})}{(h_{1},h_{2})}{(h_{1},h_{2})}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2(h_{2}-1)+h_{1},

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2}\right)}{(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2})}{(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2})}{(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2})}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(v_{2}-1)\cdot 2\binom{h}{h_{2}}+(v_{1}-1)\cdot\binom{h}{h_{1}}-1.

Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2}\right)}{(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2})}{(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2})}{(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2})}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{1}(\operatorname{poly}^{3}(h,{h}_{1},{v}_{1},{h}_{2},{v}_{2}))
.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
be a
3
-horizontal semi-web such that
h\coloneqq|\mathcal{H}|\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h_{1},h_{2}\right)}{(h_{1},h_{2})}{(h_{1},h_{2})}{(h_{1},h_{2})}
and
v\coloneqq|\mathcal{V}|\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2}\right)}{(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2})}{(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2})}{(h,h_{1},v_{1},h_{2},v_{2})}
.
Let
\mathcal{H}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{3}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}
and

\mathcal{V}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{3}\coloneqq\mathcal{V}

be decompositions of these two linkages witnessing that
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
is a 3-horizontal semi-web.
Then
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
contains one of the following:

(W1)

a
2
-horizontal web
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})}
where
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{V})}
,
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
,
|\mathcal{H}^{\prime}|\geq h_{1}
,
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is weakly
h
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
and
|\mathcal{V}^{\prime}|\geq v_{1}
, or

(W2)

some linkage
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{V})}
of order
h_{2}
and some linkage
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
of order
v_{2}
such that
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is internally disjoint from
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.
Additionally,
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
, or
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
.

Proof 4.20
.

By
4.2
,
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
contains a linkage
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{P}^{2}
of order
|\mathcal{H}|

such that

\mathcal{P}
is weakly
|\mathcal{H}|
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
,

\mathcal{P}^{2}
is internally disjoint from
\mathcal{U}^{2}\coloneqq\mathcal{V}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{3}
,

\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}=\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
,

\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
and

\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
.

For each
V_{j}\in\mathcal{V}
and each
1\leq i\leq 2
let
\mathcal{X}_{j}^{i}\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{i}
be the paths of
\mathcal{P}^{i}
which internally intersect
V_{j}
and let
\mathcal{Y}_{j}^{i}\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{i}
be the paths of
\mathcal{P}^{i}
which are internally disjoint from
V_{j}
.
Let
\mathcal{M}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
be the set of paths
V_{j}\in\mathcal{V}
for that some
i
exists such that
|\mathcal{Y}_{j}^{i}|\geq h_{2}
.
Let
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{V}\setminus\mathcal{M}
.

Case 1:

|\mathcal{M}|\leq(v_{2}-1)\cdot 2\binom{|\mathcal{H}|}{h_{2}}
.

Hence,
|\mathcal{N}|\geq|\mathcal{V}|-(v_{2}-1)\cdot 2\binom{|\mathcal{H}|}{h_{2}}\geq(v_{1}-1)\cdot\binom{|\mathcal{H}|}{h_{1}}+1
.
For each
V_{j}\in\mathcal{N}
let
\mathcal{X}_{j}=\{P^{1}\cdot P^{2}\in\mathcal{P}\mid P^{1}\in\mathcal{X}^{1}_{j}\text{ and }P^{2}\in\mathcal{X}^{2}_{j}\}
.
Since
\mathcal{X}^{i}_{j}\cup\mathcal{Y}^{i}_{j}=\mathcal{P}^{i}
and
|\mathcal{Y}^{i}_{j}|<h_{2}
for all
V_{j}\in\mathcal{N}
and all
1\leq i\leq 2
, we have that
|\mathcal{X}_{j}|\geq|\mathcal{H}|-2\cdot(h_{2}-1)\geq h_{1}
for every
V_{j}\in\mathcal{N}
.

There are at most
\binom{|\mathcal{H}|}{h_{1}}
distinct linkages
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
of order
h_{1}
.
By the pigeon-hole principle, there is some
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
of order
v_{1}
for which some
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
of order
h_{1}
exists such that
\mathcal{X}_{j}\supseteq\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
for all
V_{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.

Decompose
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
into

\mathcal{F}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{2}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
and

\mathcal{W}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{W}^{2}\coloneqq\mathcal{V}^{\prime}

such that

\mathcal{F}^{2}
are maximal subpaths of
\mathcal{P}^{2}

which do not contain
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}

and

\mathcal{W}^{1}\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{1}
.

Because
\mathcal{P}^{2}
is internally disjoint from
\mathcal{U}^{2}
,
we have that
\mathcal{F}^{2}
is disjoint from
\mathcal{W}^{2}
.
Further, by definition of
\mathcal{X}^{2}_{j}
,
every path in
\mathcal{F}^{2}
intersects every path in
\mathcal{W}^{1}
.
Finally, by definition of
\mathcal{X}^{1}_{j}
,
every path in
\mathcal{F}^{1}
intersects every path in
\mathcal{W}^{2}
.
Hence,
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})}
is a
2
-horizontal web with
|\mathcal{H}^{\prime}|=h_{1}
,
|\mathcal{V}^{\prime}|=v_{1}
, as witnessed by the decomposition above, and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is weakly
|\mathcal{H}|
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
, satisfying
item

(W1)
.

Case 2:

|\mathcal{M}|\geq(v_{2}-1)\cdot 2\binom{|\mathcal{H}|}{h_{2}}+1
.

By the pigeon-hole principle, there is some
i\in\{1,2\}
and some
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{i}
of order
h_{2}
for which there is a set
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
of order
v_{2}
such that
\mathcal{Y}^{i}_{j}\supseteq\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
for all
V_{j}\in\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.
Hence,
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is a linkage of order
h_{2}
which is internally disjoint from
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.

If
i=1
, then
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}=\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{1})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
.

If
i=2
, then
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
.

Hence,
item

(W2)
holds.

A back-linkage
\mathcal{R}
intersecting a path of well-linked sets cluster-by-cluster
provides a similar structure as a horizontal web.
Applying
lemma

4.19
we can
obtain a horizontal web
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
where
\mathcal{H}
is weakly minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
, or
we find some
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
which is disjoint from
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
.
Since the linkage
\mathcal{V}
comes from the back-linkage
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{H}
lies inside the path of well-linked sets,
we can use
lemma

4.15
to obtain
a cycle of well-linked sets.

We define

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,w,\ell\right)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\right)}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}+2{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)},

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 3({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}+1)-1,

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,w\right)}{(h,w)}{(h,w)}{(h,w)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\right)}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})},h,v,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)},2{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\right)}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\right)}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})},h,v,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)},2{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\right)}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})},h,v,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)},2{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\right)}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})},h,v,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)},2{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})},

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{m}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{m}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,w\right)}{(h,w)}{(h,w)}{(h,w)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\right)}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}{(h,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)})}.

Observe that

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,w,\ell\right)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{2}(\operatorname{poly}^{25}(h,w,\ell))
,

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{3}(\operatorname{poly}^{25}(w,\ell))
, as well as

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,w,\ell,v\right)}{(h,w,\ell,v)}{(h,w,\ell,v)}{(h,w,\ell,v)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{3}(\operatorname{poly}^{26}(h,w,\ell,v))
, and

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{m}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,w,\ell\right)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{2}(\operatorname{poly}^{25}(h,w,\ell))
.

Lemma 4.21
.

Let
w,\ell,h,v
be integers, let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
be a strict path of well-linked sets of length
\ell^{\prime}\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}
and width
w^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,w,\ell\right)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}
with a back-linkage
\mathcal{R}
of order
r\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,w,\ell,v\right)}{(h,w,\ell,v)}{(h,w,\ell,v)}{(h,w,\ell,v)}
intersecting
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
cluster-by-cluster.
Then,
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}\cup\mathscr{P}\cup\mathcal{R}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}\cup\mathscr{P}\cup\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{S}\cup\mathscr{P}\cup\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{S}\cup\mathscr{P}\cup\mathcal{R})}
contains one of the following:

(H1)

a cycle of well-linked sets of width
w
and length
\ell
, or

(H2)

an
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{m}_{\ref{lemma:coss-or-minimal-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,w,\ell\right)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}{(h,w,\ell)}
-horizontally minimal
2
-horizontal web
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
where
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
,
|\mathcal{H}|\geq h
and
|\mathcal{V}|\geq v
.

Proof 4.22
.

Let

h_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}
,

h_{2}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}
,

h_{3}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,h_{2}\right)}{(h,h_{2})}{(h,h_{2})}{(h,h_{2})}
,

w_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}
and

v_{1}=2w_{1}
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell^{\prime}}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell^{\prime}})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell^{\prime}})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell^{\prime}})}\coloneqq\mathcal{S}
and
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell^{\prime}-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell^{\prime}-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell^{\prime}-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell^{\prime}-1})}\coloneqq\mathscr{P}
.
To simplify notation, set
\mathcal{P}_{\ell^{\prime}}\coloneqq\emptyset.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be an
A(S_{0})
-
B(S_{\ell^{\prime}})
-linkage of order
h_{3}
in
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
.
By
lemma

3.6
, such a linkage exists.

Let
t_{i}=(i-1)(h_{1}+1)
for each
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.
Decompose
\mathcal{H}
into
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{3}
, where
\mathcal{H}^{i}
is the sublinkage of
\mathcal{H}
contained in
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[t_{i},t_{i+1}-1\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[t_{i},t_{i+1}-1\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[t_{i},t_{i+1}-1\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[t_{i},t_{i+1}-1\right]}
.
Decompose
\mathcal{R}
iteratively as follows.
Let
X_{0}=\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}\right)}{(\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{R})}
and let
X_{3}=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}\right)}{(\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{R})}
.
For each
0\leq i\leq 3
let
Y_{i}
be the vertices of
\mathcal{R}
such that for each
R\in\mathcal{R}
the last intersection of
R
with
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(S_{t_{i+1}-1}\cup\mathcal{P}_{t_{i+1}-1}\right)}{(S_{t_{i+1}-1}\cup\mathcal{P}_{t_{i+1}-1})}{(S_{t_{i+1}-1}\cup\mathcal{P}_{t_{i+1}-1})}{(S_{t_{i+1}-1}\cup\mathcal{P}_{t_{i+1}-1})}
lies in
Y_{i}
.
Let
X_{i}
be the successors of the vertices of
Y_{i}
in
\mathcal{R}
.
For each
1\leq i\leq 3
, let
\mathcal{R}^{i}
be the
X_{i-1}
-
X_{i}
-sublinkage of order
|\mathcal{R}|
in
\mathcal{R}
.
To simplify notation, set
\mathcal{R}^{0}
as the linkage containing only the vertices of
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{1})}{(\mathcal{R}^{1})}{(\mathcal{R}^{1})}
.

Because
\mathcal{R}
intersects
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
cluster-by-cluster, we have
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{i})}{(\mathcal{H}^{i})}{(\mathcal{H}^{i})}\cap\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}\right)}{(\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{R})}\subseteq\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{4-i}\cup\allowbreak\mathcal{R}^{3-i}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{4-i}\cup\allowbreak\mathcal{R}^{3-i})}{(\mathcal{R}^{4-i}\cup\allowbreak\mathcal{R}^{3-i})}{(\mathcal{R}^{4-i}\cup\allowbreak\mathcal{R}^{3-i})}
for all
1\leq i\leq 3
.
Hence,
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{R}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{R})}
is a
3
-horizontal semi-web.
Further,
|\mathcal{H}|=h_{3}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h,h_{2}\right)}{(h,h_{2})}{(h,h_{2})}{(h,h_{2})}
and
|\mathcal{R}|\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:c-horizontal-web-minimal-or-disjoint-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(h_{3},h,v,h_{2},v_{1}\right)}{(h_{3},h,v,h_{2},v_{1})}{(h_{3},h,v,h_{2},v_{1})}{(h_{3},h,v,h_{2},v_{1})}
.
By
lemma

4.19
, we have two cases.

Case 1:

Item

(W1)
holds.
That is,
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{R}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{R})}
contains a
2
-horizontal web
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})}
where
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{R}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{R})}
,
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
,
|\mathcal{H}^{\prime}|\geq h
,
|\mathcal{V}^{\prime}|\geq v
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is weakly
|\mathcal{H}|
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.
This satisfies
item

(H2)
.

Case 2:

Item

(W2)
holds.
That is, there is some
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{R}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{R})}{(\mathcal{H}\cup\mathcal{R})}
of order
h_{2}
and some
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
of order
v_{1}
such that
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is internally disjoint from
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.
Additionally,
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
, or
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
.
We assume, without loss of generality, that
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
holds.
The other case follows analogously by considering
\mathcal{H}^{3}
instead of
\mathcal{H}^{1}
.

We show that every path in
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
intersects
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{i}\right)}{(S_{i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{i})}{(S_{i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{i})}{(S_{i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{i})}
for every
0\leq i\leq h_{1}
.

Assume towards a contradiction that this is not the case.
As
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq A(S_{0})
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(S_{h_{1}+d}\right)}{(S_{h_{1}+d})}{(S_{h_{1}+d})}{(S_{h_{1}+d})}
for some integer
d
, there is some
0\leq j\leq h_{1}
for which some
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
exists such that
H
is an
A(S_{0})
-
V\mathchoice{\left(S_{h_{1}+d}\right)}{(S_{h_{1}+d})}{(S_{h_{1}+d})}{(S_{h_{1}+d})}
-path which does not intersect any vertex of
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}_{j}\cup\mathscr{P}_{j}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}_{j}\cup\mathscr{P}_{j})}{(\mathcal{S}_{j}\cup\mathscr{P}_{j})}{(\mathcal{S}_{j}\cup\mathscr{P}_{j})}
.

Let
j_{0}<j
be the largest index smaller than
j
such that
H
intersects
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}}\right)}{(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}})}{(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}})}{(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}})}
.
Similarly, let
j_{1}>j
be the smallest index larger than
j
such that
H
intersects
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}}\right)}{(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}})}{(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}})}{(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}})}
.
Since there is no
V\mathchoice{\left(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}}\right)}{(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}})}{(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}})}{(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}})}
-
V\mathchoice{\left(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}}\right)}{(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}})}{(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}})}{(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}})}
-path which is disjoint from
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(S_{j}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j}\right)}{(S_{j}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j})}{(S_{j}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j})}{(S_{j}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j})}
inside
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
,
H
contains a
V\mathchoice{\left(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}}\right)}{(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}})}{(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}})}{(S_{j_{0}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{0}})}
-
V\mathchoice{\left(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}}\right)}{(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}})}{(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}})}{(S_{j_{1}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j_{1}})}
-path
H_{x}
which is internally disjoint from
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
as a subpath.
Hence,
H_{x}
is a subpath of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
.
This, however, implies that
H_{x}
is a jump of length
j_{1}-j_{0}>1
, a contradiction to the initial assumption that
\mathcal{R}
intersects
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
cluster-by-cluster.

Let
S_{h_{1}}^{\prime}=S_{h_{1}}\cup\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{h_{1}}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{h_{1}})}{(\mathcal{P}_{h_{1}})}{(\mathcal{P}_{h_{1}})}
.
Clearly, the digraph
(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\coloneqq(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{h_{1}-1},S_{h_{1}}^{\prime}),\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\coloneqq(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,

\mathcal{P}_{h_{1}-1}))
is a path of well-linked sets of width
w^{\prime}\geq 2w_{1}
and length
h_{1}
.
By
4.1
, there is a partial back-linkage
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
of order
w_{1}
for
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[0,h_{1}\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[0,h_{1}\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[0,h_{1}\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[0,h_{1}\right]}
(and, hence, for
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime})}
as well) such that
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}\cap\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cup\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}\cup\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}\right)}{(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cup\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}\cup\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})})}{(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cup\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}\cup\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})})}{(\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\cup\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}\cup\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})})}
.
As
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
is weakly
m
-minimal with respect to
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}
, the back-linkage
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
is also weakly
m
-minimal with respect to
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}
.

As every path in
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
intersects
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(S_{j}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j}\right)}{(S_{j}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j})}{(S_{j}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j})}{(S_{j}\cup\mathcal{P}_{j})}
for every
0\leq j\leq h_{1}
, by
lemma

4.15
the digraph
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\cup\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\cup\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\cup\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\cup\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}
contains a cycle of well-linked sets of width
w
and length
\ell
, implying
item

(H1)
.

5
Constructing a cycle of well-linked sets

We now handle the remaining case
where we obtain a 2-horizontal web as in
lemma

4.21
item

(H2)
.
The idea is to construct a new path of well-linked sets (
section

5.1
) on the
first half
of
\mathcal{H}
in the horizontal web and then use the
last half
of
\mathcal{H}
to complete the back-linkage.
We then conclude with the proof of our main result in
section

5.2
.

5.1
A new path of well-linked sets

We start with two simple observations which allow us to reroute paths inside a web and
can be proven by a simple application of
Menger’s Theorem
(
theorem

2.2
).

Observation 5.1
.

Let
D
be a digraph and let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}
be a web where
|\mathcal{P}|=|\mathcal{Q}|
.
Then
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
is well-linked to
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}
in
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\cup\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}\cup\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P}\cup\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P}\cup\mathcal{Q})}
.

Proof 5.1
.

Let
A\subseteq\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
and
B\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}
such that
|A|=|B|
.
Since
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}
is a web, there is no
A
-
B
-separator of size less than
|A|
, as such a separator must hit
|A|
paths of
\mathcal{P}
.
Hence, by
theorem

2.2
there is an
A
-
B
-linkage of size
|A|
in
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\cup\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}\cup\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P}\cup\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P}\cup\mathcal{Q})}
.
Thus,
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
is well-linked to
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}
.

Observation 5.2
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
be a
2
-horizontal web.
Define
\mathcal{H}^{2}\coloneqq\{H_{i}^{2}\mid H_{i}\in\mathcal{H}\}
and
\mathcal{V}^{1}\coloneqq\{V_{i}^{1}\mid V_{i}\in\mathcal{V}\}
.
Then,
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
is well-linked to
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{V}^{1})}
inside
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\cup\mathcal{V}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2}\cup\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2}\cup\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2}\cup\mathcal{V}^{1})}
.

Proof 5.2
.

By definition
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1})}
is a web.
Hence, by
5.1

\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2})}
is well-linked to
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{V}^{1})}
inside
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}\cup\mathcal{V}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{2}\cup\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2}\cup\mathcal{V}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{2}\cup\mathcal{V}^{1})}
.

We also need the concepts of splits and segmentations together with the following
lemma
from
[
21
]
.

Definition 5.3

(
[
22
, Definitions 5.6 and 5.7]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\star}
be linkages and let
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{\star}
be a sublinkage of order
q
. Let
r\geq 0
.

(X1)

An
(r,q^{\prime})
-split of
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
(with respect to
\mathcal{Q}^{\star}
)
is a pair
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
of linkages of order
r=|\mathcal{P}^{\prime}|
and
q^{\prime}=|\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}|
with
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}
such that

\blacktriangleright

there is a path
P\in\mathcal{P}
and arcs
e_{1},\dots,e_{r-1}\in E\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\setminus E\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\star}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\star})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\star})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\star})}
such that
P=P_{1}e_{1}P_{2}\dots e_{r-1}P_{r}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\coloneqq(P_{1},\dots,P_{r})
,

\blacktriangleright

every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
can be divided into subpaths
Q_{1},\dots,Q_{r}
such that
Q=Q_{1}e^{\prime}_{1}\dots e^{\prime}_{r-1}Q_{r}
for suitable arcs
e^{\prime}_{1},\dots,e^{\prime}_{r-1}\in E(Q)
, and

\blacktriangleright

\emptyset\neq V(Q)\cap V(P_{i})\subseteq V(Q_{r+1-i})
, for all
1\leq i\leq r
.

(X2)

A subset
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}
of order
q^{\prime}
is a
q^{\prime}
-
segmentation of
P\in\mathcal{P}
(with respect to
\mathcal{Q}^{\star}
)
if

\blacktriangleright

there are arcs
e_{1},\dots,e_{q^{\prime}-1}\in E\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}-E\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\star}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\star})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\star})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\star})}
with
P=P_{1}e_{1}\dots P_{q^{\prime}-1}e_{q^{\prime}-1}P_{q^{\prime}}
, for suitable subpaths
P_{1},\dots,P_{q^{\prime}}
, and

\blacktriangleright

\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
can be ordered as
(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{q^{\prime}})
and
V\mathchoice{\left(Q_{i}\right)}{(Q_{i})}{(Q_{i})}{(Q_{i})}\cap V\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(P_{i}\right)}{(P_{i})}{(P_{i})}{(P_{i})}
.

(X3)

An
(r,q^{\prime})
-segmentation (with respect to
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
)
is a pair
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
where
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is a linkage of order
r
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is a linkage of order
q^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is a
q^{\prime}
-segmentation (with respect to
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
) of every path
P_{i}
into segments
P^{i}_{1}e_{1}P^{i}_{2}\dots e_{q^{\prime}-1}P^{i}_{q^{\prime}}
.

(X4)

A segmentation
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
is
ordered
if for all
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
the order
\mathchoice{\left(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{q^{\prime}}\right)}{(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{q^{\prime}})}{(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{q^{\prime}})}{(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{q^{\prime}})}
given by the
q^{\prime}
-segmentation of
P_{i}
is the same.
We say that
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
is an (ordered)
(r,q^{\prime})
-segmentation of
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
if
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}
and every path in
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is a subpath of a path in
\mathcal{P}
.

An
(r,q)
-split
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
or an
(r,q)
-segmentation
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})

is well-linked if
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}
is well-linked to
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{Q})}
.

Observe that every
(r,q)
-segmentation is an ordered
(r,q)
-web and
that every
(r,q)
-split is a folded ordered
(r,q)
-web.

In order to construct a path of well-linked sets on the
first half
of
\mathcal{H}
,
we need to construct a split or a segmentation which is connected in an adequate way
to the
last half
of
\mathcal{H}
.
Towards this end, we require the following statements from
[
21
,
16
]
.

Lemma 5.4

(
[
21
, Corollary 5.12]
)
.

Let
H
be a digraph and let
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
be a linkage in
H
, and let
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{*}
be a linkage of order
q
.
Let
P\subseteq H
be a path intersecting every path in
\mathcal{Q}
.
Let
c\geq 0
be such that for every arc
e\in E\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\setminus E\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}^{*}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q}^{*})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{*})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{*})}
there are no
c
pairwise vertex-disjoint paths in
H-e
from
P_{1}
to
P_{2}
, where
P=P_{1}\cdot e\cdot P_{2}
.
For all
s,r\geq 0
, if
q\geq(r+c)\cdot s
, then

(R1)

there is an
s
-segmentation
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}
of
P
with respect to
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
or

(R2)

a
(2,r)
-split
((P_{1},P_{2}),\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime})
of
(P,\mathcal{Q})
with respect to
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
.

The following is an adaptation of the construction in the proof of
[
21
, Lemma 5.15]
, which is in turn based on some results of
[
27
]
.
We obtain the same bounds as
[
21
]
, but we require somewhat different properties from the segmentation obtained.
In particular, we want the paths of the segmentation to be in the end of the linkage
\mathcal{H}^{1}
in our horizontal web
(\mathcal{H}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2}=\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
.
This will allow us to go from the last cluster of the path of well-linked sets to
\mathcal{H}^{2}
without
intersecting the new path of well-linked sets.

The idea of the proof is to iteratively apply
lemma

5.4
to each path of
\mathcal{P}
.
If we can split one path many times, the we obtain the desired
(y,q)
-split.
Otherwise, we obtain many different paths of
\mathcal{P}
which contain an
(x,q)
-segmentation.
By taking the subpath at the end of path in
\mathcal{P}
which is in the segmentation,
we obtain the properties we need later.

We define

\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:web-to-split-or-segmentation-at-end}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:web-to-split-or-segmentation-at-end}}}\mathchoice{\left(q,c,z\right)}{(q,c,z)}{(q,c,z)}{(q,c,z)}\coloneqq(q(c+1))^{2^{z}}(2^{2^{z}-1})

and note that

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:web-to-split-or-segmentation-at-end}}}\mathchoice{\left(q,c,z\right)}{(q,c,z)}{(q,c,z)}{(q,c,z)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{2}(\operatorname{poly}^{2}(q,c,z))
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
c,x,y,q,q^{\prime}\geq 0
and
p\geq x
be integers.
Let
\mathcal{W}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}
be a
\mathchoice{\left(p,q^{\prime}\right)}{(p,q^{\prime})}{(p,q^{\prime})}{(p,q^{\prime})}
-web where
\mathcal{P}
is weakly
c
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{Q}
.
If
q^{\prime}\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:web-to-split-or-segmentation-at-end}}}\mathchoice{\left(q,c,xy\right)}{(q,c,xy)}{(q,c,xy)}{(q,c,xy)}
, then there is some
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}
such that
\mathcal{W}
contains one of the following

(S1)

a
\mathchoice{\left(y,q\right)}{(y,q)}{(y,q)}{(y,q)}
-split
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}
of
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}
or

(S2)

an
\mathchoice{\left(x,q\right)}{(x,q)}{(x,q)}{(x,q)}
-segmentation
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}
of
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}
with
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
.

Proof 5.6
.

For all
0\leq i\leq xy
, we define values
q_{i}
inductively as follows.
We set
q_{xy}\coloneqq q
and
q_{i-1}\coloneqq q_{i}\cdot(q_{i}+c)
.
We first show that
q_{0}\leq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:web-to-split-or-segmentation-at-end}}}\mathchoice{\left(q,c,xy\right)}{(q,c,xy)}{(q,c,xy)}{(q,c,xy)}
.

Claim 1
.

q_{i}\leq(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-i}}(2^{2^{xy-i}-1})
for all
0\leq i\leq xy

Proof 5.7

(Proof)
.

Clearly
q_{xy}=q\leq q(c+1)
.
Assume the inequality holds from
xy
to
i>0
.
By definition of
q_{i-1}
we obtain

\displaystyle q_{i-1}

\displaystyle=q_{i}\cdot(q_{i}+c)

\displaystyle\leq(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-i}}(2^{2^{xy-i}-1})\cdot((q(c+1))^{2^{xy-i}}(2^{2^{xy-i}-1})+c)

\displaystyle=((q(c+1))^{2^{xy-i}}(2^{2^{xy-i}-1}))^{2}+(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-i}}(2^{2^{xy-i}-1})c

\displaystyle=(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-(i-1)}}(2^{2^{xy-(i-1)}-2})+(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-i}}(2^{2^{xy-i}-1})c

\displaystyle\leq(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-(i-1)}}(2^{2^{xy-(i-1)}-2}+2^{2^{xy-i}-1})

\displaystyle=(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-(i-1)}}(2^{2^{xy-i}-1}\cdot 2^{2^{xy-i}-1}+2^{2^{xy-i}-1})

\displaystyle=(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-(i-1)}}(2^{2^{xy-i}-1}\cdot(2^{2^{xy-i}-1}+1))

\displaystyle\leq(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-(i-1)}}(2^{2^{xy-i}-1}\cdot 2^{2^{xy-i}})

\displaystyle=(q(c+1))^{2^{xy-(i-1)}}(2^{2^{xy-(i-1)}-1}).

Hence, by
1
, we have
q_{0}\leq q^{\prime}\leq(q(c+1))^{2^{xy}}(2^{2^{xy}-1})={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q^{\prime}}_{\ref{lemma:web-to-split-or-segmentation-at-end}}}\mathchoice{\left(q,c,xy\right)}{(q,c,xy)}{(q,c,xy)}{(q,c,xy)}
.

For each
0\leq i\leq xy
we construct a tuple
\mathcal{M}_{i}\coloneqq(\mathcal{P}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{i},\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i},\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i})
satisfying all of the following

(M1)

\mathcal{Q}^{i}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{*}
is a linkage of order
q_{i}
and
\mathcal{P}^{i}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
is a linkage such that, if there is no
P\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{i}_{\mathsf{seg}}
with
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
, then
|\mathcal{P}^{i}\cup\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}|\geq x
,

(M2)

(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{i})
is a
(y_{i},q_{i})
-split of
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
and

(M3)

(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{i})
is an
(x_{i},q_{i})
-segmentation of
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}
where
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i})}{(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i})}{(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i})}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
.

Furthermore, for all
i
,
(\mathcal{P}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{i})
has linkedness
c
and
V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{i})}{(\mathcal{P}^{i})}{(\mathcal{P}^{i})}\cap V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i})}{(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i})}{(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i})}=\emptyset
.
Recall that, in particular, this means that the paths in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}
are the subpaths of a single path in
\mathcal{P}
that is split by arcs
e\in E\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\setminus E\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}^{*}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q}^{*})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{*})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{*})}
.

We first set
\mathcal{P}^{0}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}
,
\mathcal{Q}^{0}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}^{*}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{0}\coloneqq\emptyset
,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{0}\coloneqq\emptyset
.
Clearly, this satisfies the conditions
item

(M1)
,
item

(M2)
and
item

(M3)
defined above.

We do the following on step
i+1\geq 1
.
If
|\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}|\geq y
or if
|\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}|\geq x
, stop the construction.
Otherwise, proceed as follows.

We first set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}
.
If there is no
P\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}
such that
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
, we choose a path
P\in\mathcal{P}^{i}
and set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{\prime}=\{P\}
and
\mathcal{P}^{i+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}^{i}\setminus\{P\}
.
Clearly,
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
.

Otherwise, if there is some
P\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}
with
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
, we set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}
and
\mathcal{P}^{i+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}^{i}
.

Now, let
P\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{\prime}
with
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
.
We apply
lemma

5.4
to
P,\mathcal{Q}^{i}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
setting
r=s=q_{i+1}
in the statement of the lemma.
If
item

(R1)
holds and there is a
q_{i+1}
-segmentation
\mathcal{Q}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{i}
of
P
with respect to
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
, we set

\mathcal{Q}^{i+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}_{1},\qquad\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}\cup\{P\}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}.

Otherwise,
item

(R2)
holds and there is a
\mathchoice{\left(2,q_{i+1}\right)}{(2,q_{i+1})}{(2,q_{i+1})}{(2,q_{i+1})}
-split
\mathchoice{\left(\mathchoice{\left(P_{1},P_{2}\right)}{(P_{1},P_{2})}{(P_{1},P_{2})}{(P_{1},P_{2})},\mathcal{Q}_{2}\right)}{(\mathchoice{\left(P_{1},P_{2}\right)}{(P_{1},P_{2})}{(P_{1},P_{2})}{(P_{1},P_{2})},\mathcal{Q}_{2})}{(\mathchoice{\left(P_{1},P_{2}\right)}{(P_{1},P_{2})}{(P_{1},P_{2})}{(P_{1},P_{2})},\mathcal{Q}_{2})}{(\mathchoice{\left(P_{1},P_{2}\right)}{(P_{1},P_{2})}{(P_{1},P_{2})}{(P_{1},P_{2})},\mathcal{Q}_{2})}
where
\mathcal{Q}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{i}
.
Then we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{i+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}\setminus\{P\})\cup\{P_{1},P_{2}\}.

If there is no
P\in|\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i+1}|
with
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
, then we obtained a segmentation and so added a path to
\mathcal{S}^{i+1}_{\mathsf{seg}}
.
As
item

(M1)
holds for
i
, we have that
|\mathcal{P}^{i+1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i+1}|\geq x
in this case.

It is easily verified that the conditions
item

(M1)
,
item

(M2)
and
item

(M3)
are maintained for
\mathcal{P}^{i+1}
,
\mathcal{Q}^{i+1}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i+1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i+1}
.
In particular, the linkedness
c
of
(\mathcal{P}^{i+1},\mathcal{Q}^{i+1})
is preserved as deleting or splitting paths cannot increase forward connectivity.
This concludes the construction.

Note that in the construction, after every
y
steps, either
item

(R2)
holds after every application of
lemma

5.4
, and so we find a set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}
of size
y
or
item

(R1)
holds in at least one of the
y
steps, and so we add a path to
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}
.
Whenever this happens, we take a new path
P\in\mathcal{P}^{i}
in the next iteration, which always exists because of
item

(M1)
.

Hence, in the construction above, in each step we either increase
x_{i}
and add the path
P
with
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
to
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}
or we increase
y_{i}
.
After at most
i\leq xy
steps, either we have constructed a set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}
of order
x
or a set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}
of order
y
.

Because
item

(M2)
holds, if we found a set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i}
of order
y
, then we can choose any set
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{i}
of order
q
and
(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
satisfies
item

(S1)
.

If, instead, we get a set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}\coloneqq\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}^{i}
of order
x^{\prime}\geq x
, then, by
item

(M3)
,

(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}},\mathcal{Q}^{i})
is an
(x,q)
-segmentation of
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
such that
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}})}{(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}})}{(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}})}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
, satisfying
item

(S2)
.

Finally, it is easily seen that if
\mathcal{W}
is well-linked then so is
(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{split}}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
(in case
item

(S1)
holds) or
(\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{seg}},\mathcal{Q}^{i})
(in case
item

(S2)
holds).

In order to use the results of

,
we use the split or the segmentation obtained from

to construct a folded ordered web or an ordered web.

.
