Proof 5.9
.

Let
x_{1}=2x-1
.

We apply
lemma

5.5
to
\mathcal{W}
.
If
item

(S1)
holds, then by
5.3
we obtain a folded ordered
(q,y)
-web and
item

(O1)
holds.
Otherwise,
item

(S2)
holds and we obtain an
(x_{1},q)
-segmentation
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{1},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{1},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{1},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{1},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}
of
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
such that
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{1})}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
.

Recursively define
q_{i}
by
q_{x_{1}}=q^{\prime\prime}
and
q_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{len}_{\ref{thm:erdos_szekeres}}}\mathchoice{\left(q_{i+1},q_{i+1}\right)}{(q_{i+1},q_{i+1})}{(q_{i+1},q_{i+1})}{(q_{i+1},q_{i+1})}
.
We show that
q_{i}\leq(q^{\prime\prime})^{2^{x_{1}-i}}+1
for all
1\leq i\leq x_{1}
.
Clearly,
q_{x_{1}}=q^{\prime\prime}\leq(q^{\prime\prime})^{2^{0}}+1
.
By definition, for an arbitrary
1\leq i\leq x_{1}
we have

\displaystyle q_{i}

\displaystyle=(q_{i+1}-1)^{2}+1

\displaystyle\leq((q^{\prime\prime})^{2^{x_{1}-i-1}}+1-1)^{2}+1

\displaystyle=(q^{\prime\prime})^{2^{x_{1}-i}}+1.

Hence,
q\geq q_{1}
.

We construct a set
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
as follows.
Let
\{P^{1}_{1},P^{1}_{2},\dots,P^{1}_{x_{1}}\}=\mathcal{P}^{1}
be an arbitrary ordering of the paths in
\mathcal{P}^{1}
.
Set
\mathcal{Q}_{1}=\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
and then iterate from
2
to
x_{1}
, constructing a set
\mathcal{Q}_{i}
of size
q_{i}
.

On step
i\leq x_{1}
, consider the ordering
\preceq_{i-1}
of the paths in
\mathcal{Q}_{i-1}
according to their occurrence along
P^{1}_{i-1}
.
By
theorem

2.1
, there is a
\mathcal{Q}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{i-1}
of order at least
q_{i}
such that
P^{1}_{i}
intersects
\mathcal{Q}_{i}
in order or in reverse with respect to
\preceq_{i-1}
.
Since
\mathcal{Q}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{i-1}
, we have that each
P^{1}_{j}\in\mathcal{P}^{1}
with
j\leq i
also intersects
\mathcal{Q}_{i}
in order or in reverse with respect to
\preceq_{i-1}
.

After
x_{1}
steps, we set
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}_{x_{1}}
.
By construction, there is an ordering
\preceq
of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
such that each
P^{1}_{i}\in\mathcal{P}^{1}
intersects
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
in order or in reverse with respect to
\preceq
.

By the pigeon-hole principle, there is some
\mathcal{P}^{2}\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{1}
of order at least
x
such that every path in
\mathcal{P}^{2}
intersects the paths of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
in the same order.
Hence,
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{2},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{2},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime})}
is an ordered
(x,q^{\prime\prime})
-web where
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}{(\mathcal{P}^{2})}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
, satisfying
item

(O2)
.

We consider two cases when constructing a path of well-linked sets or a path of order-linked sets
from the
first half
of
\mathcal{H}
in our horizontal web
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{2})}{(\mathcal{H}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{2})}
.

In the first case, we obtain a folded ordered web
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
from
lemma

5.8
, where
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{1}
.
In this case, we obtain a
forward linkage

\mathcal{L}
which is contained in
\mathcal{V}^{2}
.
This will allow us to use
\mathcal{V}^{1}
to build a back-linkage which is disjoint from
\mathcal{L}
, and then use
lemma

4.15
to obtain a cycle of well-linked sets.

In the second case, we obtain an ordered web
(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})
which ends on
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{1})}{(\mathcal{H}^{1})}
.
We can then use the subpaths of
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
after the last path of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
to construct a
linkage to
\mathcal{H}^{2}
which does not intersect the path of order-linked sets constructed from the ordered web.
We will then need to use
\mathcal{V}^{1}
in order to construct the back-linkage.
As
\mathcal{V}^{1}
may intersect our path of order-linked sets, we want to be able to restrict how these intersections happen.
The idea will be to use paths of
\mathcal{V}^{2}
leading to each cluster in order to
obtain a large linkage which contradicts the assumption that
\mathcal{H}
is weakly minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
.

Lemma

5.11
below provides the base cases for our argument of
lemma

5.13
later.
The case
item

(E1)
below follows immediately from
lemma

5.8
item

(O1)
.
For

, we will need to construct a routing temporal digraph and use the framework from
section

3
in order
to construct the path of order-linked sets with the desired properties.

We require the following lemma for our proof.

Proof 5.12
.

We define

h_{2}=(w_{2})^{2}-1
,

h_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w_{1},\ell_{1}\right)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1})}{(w_{1},\ell_{1})}{(w_{1},\ell_{1})}

\ell_{4}=w_{2}\ell_{2}
,

By

, there is some
\mathcal{H}^{1}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
such that one of the following cases hold:

holds.

for which

\mathchoice{\left(v_{1},h_{1}\right)}{(v_{1},h_{1})}{(v_{1},h_{1})}{(v_{1},h_{1})}

\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}^{1},\mathcal{H}^{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{V}^{1},\mathcal{H}^{1})}{(\mathcal{V}^{1},\mathcal{H}^{1})}{(\mathcal{V}^{1},\mathcal{H}^{1})}

Item

(O2)
holds.

That is,

\mathchoice{\left(h_{2},v_{2}\right)}{(h_{2},v_{2})}{(h_{2},v_{2})}{(h_{2},v_{2})}

To build our cycle of well-linked sets, we will first use a construction from

in order to

in our

from

item

Further, we also want some path

which can be used
when we instead obtain a path of order-linked sets from

item

(E2)
.
We can then use the linkage

from

to the path of order-linked sets.
Using the clusters, we reach the paths of
\mathcal{V}^{1}
which intersect our

In this way, we can obtain a large linkage avoiding an arc of

if

intersects too many clusters.

On the other hand, if

is disjoint from most clusters,
then we can obtain a back-linkage as in the precondition of
lemma

4.2
.
This can then be used to construct the cycle of well-linked sets as desired.

.

Proof 5.14
.

We define

\displaystyle z_{1}\coloneqq c+1,\qquad q_{1}\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{lemma:well-linked-poss-with-disjoint-forward-linkage-implies-coss}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)},\qquad\ell_{4}\coloneqq\ell-1,

\displaystyle v_{2}\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:1-horizontal-web-double-side-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w_{1},\ell_{1},w_{3},\ell_{2},c\right)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},w_{3},\ell_{2},c)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},w_{3},\ell_{2},c)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1},w_{3},\ell_{2},c)},\quad v_{1}\coloneqq(v_{2}-1)(m_{2}+1)\binom{h_{1}}{m_{1}}+1.

Observe that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:coss-inside-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}=m_{1}+m_{2}+1
and
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:coss-inside-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell,c\right)}{(w,\ell,c)}{(w,\ell,c)}{(w,\ell,c)}=v_{1}+{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:coss-inside-2-horizontal-web}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}c
.

Decompose

into

in

.

Let

.

.

\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}

.
Further,
\mathcal{V}^{*}
satisfies the following by construction.

(V1)

Let

be an arbitrary decomposition of a path in
\mathcal{V}^{*}
.
If

.

Let

and
\mathcal{V}^{2}

and

.
Note that

.

For each subpath

of
\mathcal{V}^{*}
which contains some path of
\mathcal{V}^{4}
as a subpath, define a linear ordering
\preceq_{V_{i}}
on

with

.

, every

is a linear ordering.
Define

Since

5.2
The Directed Grid Theorem

We are now ready to prove our main theorems.
We state our main result in terms of cylindrical grids, walls and cycles of well-linked sets, as each may be useful in a different setting.

We define

6
Conclusion

While we significantly improved the bounds for the Directed Grid Theorem, the functions are still very large and likely far from optimal.
Determining the best bounds is an interesting open question.

Our modular approach allows us to break the question above down into smaller parts, facilitating further improvements to the Directed Grid Theorem.
In particular, the concept of 2-horizontal webs played a central role in our proof, and studying this object more closely may lead to new, valuable insights.
For example, consider the construction given in
fig.

11
and the following simple observation.

Figure 11
:
An illustration of the construction in the proof of
6.1
.
The area with a gray background corresponds to where the linkage
\mathcal{H}
is,
with the dashed line marking where
\mathcal{H}_{1}
ends and where
\mathcal{H}_{2}
starts.
The dashed gray line outside of the gray area delimits where
\mathcal{V}_{1}
ends and where

\mathcal{V}_{2}
starts.
The light gray arcs and vertices are not used by the horizontal web.

Observation 6.1
.

Every cylindrical grid of order
2k
contains a 2-horizontal web
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
where
\mathcal{H}
is minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
and
|\mathcal{H}|=k=|\mathcal{V}|
.

Proof 6.1
.

Let
C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{2k}
be the cycles and let
P_{1},P_{2},\ldots,P_{4k}
be the paths of a cylindrical grid of order
2k
.

Let
\mathcal{H}=\{P_{1},P_{3},\ldots,P_{2k-1}\}
and let
\mathcal{H}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{2}=\mathcal{H}
such that
\mathcal{H}_{2}
contains the subpaths of
\mathcal{H}
intersecting
C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{k}
and
\mathcal{H}_{1}
contains the subpaths of
\mathcal{H}
intersecting
C_{k+1},C_{k+2},\ldots,C_{2k}
.

Let
\mathcal{V}_{1}
be the subpaths of
C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{k}
from
V\mathchoice{\left(P_{1}\right)}{(P_{1})}{(P_{1})}{(P_{1})}
to
V\mathchoice{\left(P_{2k}\right)}{(P_{2k})}{(P_{2k})}{(P_{2k})}
.
Note that
\mathcal{V}_{1}
intersects all paths of
\mathcal{H}_{2}
.

Construct
\mathcal{V}_{2}
as follows.
Let
\mathcal{R}
be the linkage from
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}_{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{V}_{1})}{(\mathcal{V}_{1})}{(\mathcal{V}_{1})}
to
\bigcup_{i=k+1}^{2k}V\mathchoice{\left(C_{i}\right)}{(C_{i})}{(C_{i})}{(C_{i})}\cap V\mathchoice{\left(P_{4k}\right)}{(P_{4k})}{(P_{4k})}{(P_{4k})}
.
This linkage exists since we can use each of the
k
paths
P_{2k},P_{2k+2},\ldots,P_{4k-2}
in order to reroute one path from a vertex of
C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{k}
to a vertex of
C_{k+1},C_{k+2},\ldots,C_{2k}
.
We construct
\mathcal{V}_{2}
by first taking
\mathcal{R}
and then the subpaths of
C_{k+1},C_{k+2},\ldots,C_{2k}
from
P_{4k-2}
to
P_{2k-1}
.
Note that
\mathcal{V}_{2}
intersects all paths of
\mathcal{H}_{1}
without intersecting any path of
\mathcal{H}_{2}
.
Let
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{2}
.
Hence,
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
is a 2-horizontal web where
\mathcal{H}
is minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
.
Further,
|\mathcal{H}|=|\mathcal{V}|=k
.

In
lemma

5.13
, we essentially prove that the converse holds for some elementary function.
Do we obtain a polynomial bound for the other direction as well?
Alternatively, one can start with the weaker question, asking for a cycle of well-linked sets instead.

Question 6.2
.

Are there polynomials
h,v
such that every 2-horizontal web
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
where
\mathcal{H}
is weakly
c
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
with
|\mathcal{H}|\geq h(w,\ell,c)
and
|\mathcal{V}|\geq v(w,\ell,c)
contains a cycle of well-linked sets of length
\ell
and width
w
?

Similarly, it is not difficult to see that a 2-horizontal web has a large directed treewidth.
To see this, recall the definition of
haven
from
[
19
]
.
A haven of order
w
in
D
is a function
\beta
assigning to every set
Z\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}
with
|Z|<w
the vertex-set of a strong component of
D\setminus Z
in such a way that if
Z^{\prime}\subseteq Z\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}
, then
\beta\mathchoice{\left(Z\right)}{(Z)}{(Z)}{(Z)}\subseteq\beta\mathchoice{\left(Z^{\prime}\right)}{(Z^{\prime})}{(Z^{\prime})}{(Z^{\prime})}
.
Johnson, Robertson, Seymour and Thomas
[
19
]
prove that, if a digraph contains a haven of order
w
, then its directed treewidth is at least
w-1
.

Observation 6.2
.

Every 2-horizontal web
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
contains a haven of order
\min(h,v)
, where
h=|\mathcal{{H}}|
and
v=|\mathcal{V}|
.
In particular,
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
has directed treewidth at least
\min(h,v)-1
.

Proof 6.3
.

Let
w=\min(h,v)
.
Let
Z\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}
be some set of size less than
w
.
Let
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
such that neither
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
nor
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
contain any vertex of
Z
.
Let
\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime}
be the sublinkage of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
where the paths start and end inside
V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}
.
Similarly, let
\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime}
be the sublinkage of
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
where the paths start and end inside
V\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime})}{(\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime})}{(\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime})}
.
Observe that
(\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime})
is also a 2-horizontal web.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{2}
be the partition of
\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime}
and let
\mathcal{V}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{2}
be the partition of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime}
witnessing that
(\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime})
is a 2-horizontal web.

As
\mathcal{V}_{1}
intersects all paths of
\mathcal{H}_{2}
and
\mathcal{V}_{2}
intersects all paths of
\mathcal{H}_{1}
, it is easy to see that the digraph
D^{\prime}
induced by
\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime}\cup\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime}
is strongly connected.
Hence, we assign
\beta\mathchoice{\left(Z\right)}{(Z)}{(Z)}{(Z)}=D^{\prime}
.
Clearly,
\beta
is a haven of order
w
.

Lemmas

4.21
and
1.1
essentially imply that the converse statement holds with an elementary function between the directed treewidth and the size of the 2-horizontal web.
Can we also obtain a polynomial bound?

Question 6.4
.

Is there a polynomial
w
such that every digraph
D
with
\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}\geq w(h,v,c)
contains a 2-horizontal web
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
where
\mathcal{H}
is weakly
c
-minimal with respect to
\mathcal{V}
with
|\mathcal{H}|\geq h
and
|\mathcal{V}|\geq v
?

If there is a polynomial bound for the Directed Grid Theorem, the answer to both questions above is “yes”.
Conversely, obtaining good functions for either question (even if not polynomial) would immediately lead to better bounds for the Directed Grid Theorem by applying our framework.
Hence, if one intends to improve the bounds of the Directed Grid Theorem further, it is only natural to consider both questions above, as they are necessary and sufficient steps towards this end.
